<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Use of Estimates</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The preparation of the Company&#146;s financial statements in
conformity with accounting principles generally accepted in the
United States of America requires management to make estimates
and assumptions that affect the reported amounts of assets and
liabilities at the balance sheet dates and the reported amounts
of revenue and expenses during the reporting periods. Actual
results could differ from those estimates.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Net Loss Per Share</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In accordance with SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;128, <I>Earnings Per
Share,</I> basic and dilutive net loss per share has been
computed using the weighted-average number of shares of common
stock outstanding during the period. All potentially dilutive
securities have not been included in the calculation of net loss
per share due to their anti-dilutive effect.
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">39

<P><HR noshade><P>
<H5 align="left" style="page-break-before:always"><A HREF="#tocpage">Table of Contents</A></H5><P>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">
<B>Knova Software, Inc.</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Notes to Consolidated Financial
Statements&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 24pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Other Comprehensive Income (Loss)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Other comprehensive income (loss) consists of net unrealized
gains and losses from securities available-for-sale in 2005 and
2004 and foreign currency translation adjustments in 2003.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Impact of Recently Issued Accounting Standards</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In March 2005, the SEC issued Staff Accounting
Bulletin&nbsp;No.&nbsp;107, <I>Share-Based Payment
</I>(&#147;SAB&nbsp;107&#148;), which provides interpretive
guidance related to the interaction between SFAS&nbsp;123R and
certain SEC rules and regulations, as well as provides the SEC
staff&#146;s views regarding the valuation of share-based
payment arrangements. The Company is currently assessing the
impact of SAB&nbsp;107 on its implementation and adoption of
SFAS&nbsp;123R.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In May 2005, the FASB issued SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;154
<I>Accounting Changes and Error Corrections, a replacement of
APB Opinion No.&nbsp;20 and FASB Statement No.&nbsp;3
</I>(SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;154). SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;154 requires
that all voluntary changes in accounting principles be
recognized as a retroactive restatement of all periods presented
except when retroactive restatement is impracticable. In
addition, SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;154 also requires that a change in
depreciation method be accounted for as a change in accounting
estimate that is affected by a change in accounting principle,
which would be accounted for in the period of change if the
change affects that period only and also in future periods if
the change affects both the period of change and future periods.
SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;154 also eliminates paragraph&nbsp;4 of
APB&nbsp;20, which specifies that APB&nbsp;20 does not apply to
changes made to comply with new pronouncements. This Statement
will require that any change in an accounting principle made as
a result of adopting a new pronouncement be reported following
the same guidance as that for voluntary changes in accounting
principles, unless the new pronouncement&#146;s transition
provisions require another method. SFAS&nbsp;No.&nbsp;154 is
effective for years beginning after December&nbsp;15, 2005.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Liquidity and Capital Resources</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Historically, the Company has satisfied its cash requirements
primarily through private placements of convertible preferred
stock and common stock, an initial public offering and
incurrence of debt. For the year ended December&nbsp;31, 2005,
the Company incurred a net loss of $5,094,821 and had negative
cash flow from operations of $4,997,983. The Company&#146;s
ability to continue as a business in its present form is largely
dependent on the Company&#146;s ability to generate additional
revenues, maintain its overall operating expenses and achieve
profitability and positive cash flows. The Company believes that
it has the ability to do so and plans to fund 2006 operations
through operating revenues, cash, cash equivalents and
investment balances and borrowings under its line of credit. As
of December 31, 2005, the Company had $12.2 million in cash,
cash equivalents and investments.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; color: #000000; background: #ffffff;"></DIV>

<TR>

    <TD width="92%"></TD>
</TR>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>Note&nbsp;3.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Fair Value of Financial Instruments</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The carrying amounts of the Company&#146;s financial instruments
consisting principally of cash and cash equivalents, short term
investments, accounts receivable, payables and debt approximate
their fair values at December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004.
</DIV>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>Note&nbsp;6.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Debt</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Credit Facilities</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
On December&nbsp;21, 2005, the Company entered into a Loan and
Security Agreement (the &#147;Agreement&#148;) with Silicon
Valley Bank (the &#147;Bank&#148;). The Agreement provides for
the Bank to make advances to the Company until December&nbsp;19,
2007, of up to $4,000,000 in the aggregate, as a revolving
facility and to provide an equipment loan of up to $500,000. As
part of the revolving loan, the Bank may issue up to $3,000,000
in aggregate of letters of credit, provide cash management
services and provide entry into foreign exchange forward
contracts. Pursuant to the Agreement, the loans will bear
interest at the prime rate plus one-half to one percent,
depending on the Company&#146;s quick asset ratio. All loans
under the Agreement will be due by December&nbsp;19, 2007,
unless accelerated or otherwise paid prior to that time. The
Company&#146;s obligations under the Agreement are secured by
certain assets of the Company (excluding its intellectual
property) and the Company has agreed not to pledge its
intellectual property rights during the term of the loan. The
Company is subject to customary affirmative and negative
covenants under the Agreement, including restrictions on the
ability to incur additional indebtedness and maintenance of a
minimum tangible net worth. The Agreement includes customary
events of default, including bankruptcy events, entry of
judgments against the Company and occurrence of material adverse
changes. Upon the occurrence of an event of default, the Bank
may cease to provide advances. As of December&nbsp;31, 2005,
there was $3.6&nbsp;million borrowed against the Agreement.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Convertible Notes</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
On April&nbsp;1, 2002, the Company signed a binding commitment
letter for the sale of convertible notes. The closing of the
transaction took place in two tranches on May 6 and
June&nbsp;19, 2002 with total proceeds of $2,975,000 being
received, net of transaction costs of $275,000.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The convertible notes were originally to mature 18&nbsp;months
from the closing date, bore interest at 10%&nbsp;per annum, and
were convertible at any time at the option of the holder, into
shares of the Company&#146;s common stock at a conversion price
of $3.00&nbsp;per share. Interest was payable in cash or
additional convertible notes, at the option of the Company. The
convertible notes were senior unsecured obligations that ranked
senior to all future subordinated indebtedness, pari passu to
all existing and future senior, unsecured indebtedness and
subordinated to all existing and future senior secured
indebtedness. While the notes were outstanding, the Company was
restricted from paying or declaring dividends on common stock,
making any other distribution on common stock, or repurchasing
or redeeming any shares of common stock.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In accordance with
EITF&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">00-27,</FONT>
<I>Application of EITF Issue No.&nbsp;98-5, &#147;Accounting for
Convertible Securities with Beneficial Conversion Features or
Contingently Adjustable Conversion Ratios,&#148; to Certain
Convertible Instruments,</I> the Company recognized in 2002 a
beneficial conversion feature (&#147;BCF&#148;) in the aggregate
amount of $2,225,000 as the difference between the market value
of the Company&#146;s common stock on the commitment date and
the conversion price of the convertible notes, reduced for the
investors&#146; transaction costs. The BCF was recorded as an
increase in additional paid in capital and a discount on debt in
the accompanying 2002 consolidated balance sheet. Additionally,
the Company incurred total legal and other expenses of
approximately $54,000 related to the transaction, which was also
recorded as a discount on debt. The aggregate discount was being
amortized as interest expense over the 18&nbsp;month term of the
convertible notes.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
On March&nbsp;31, 2003, the noteholders agreed to an amendment
to the original notes. The amendment reduced the conversion
price from $3.00&nbsp;per share to $2.50&nbsp;per share and
extended the term of the notes until July&nbsp;15, 2004.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
On October&nbsp;31, 2003, April&nbsp;30, 2003 and
October&nbsp;31, 2002, interest payments were due on the
convertible notes and were paid by issuing additional
convertible notes in the amounts of $177,748, $169,284 and
$135,681, respectively. These additional convertible notes had
the same terms as the amended convertible
</DIV>

<P align="center" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">42

Statements&nbsp;&#151;&nbsp;(Continued)</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
notes. The Company recognized additional BCF of $232,672 and

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;

    <B>Restructuring Charges</B></TD>
</TR>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
During the first quarter 2005, in connection with the
integration of its acquisition of Kanisa, the Company approved a
plan to streamline its operations. The workforce reduction of 21
employees consisted of one executive, three from general and
administrative, nine from research and development, three from
professional services and five from sales. During the quarter
ended March&nbsp;31, 2005, the Company recorded restructuring
charges of approximately $1.4&nbsp;million, including stock
based compensation of $242,000 for an executive. As of
December&nbsp;31, 2005, $261,252 in severance costs remain to be
paid to terminated employees over the next year.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Common Stock and Preferred Stock</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company has two classes of capital stock consisting of
common stock and preferred stock. As of December&nbsp;31, 2005,
a total of 50,000,000&nbsp;shares were authorized for common
stock and 5,000,000&nbsp;shares were authorized for preferred
stock.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company has reserved 3,158,799&nbsp;shares of common stock
as of December&nbsp;31, 2005. Of this total,
2,090,514&nbsp;shares are reserved for exercise of stock options
and 1,068,285&nbsp;shares are reserved for exercise of warrants.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;

December&nbsp;31, 2005.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Stock Split</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
At the Company&#146;s 2004 annual meeting of stockholders on
December&nbsp;7, 2004, the stockholders approved an amendment to
the Company&#146;s Certificate of Incorporation to effect a
reverse stock split of its common stock and to grant its board
of directors the authority until June&nbsp;30, 2006 to set the
ratio for the reverse split or to not complete the reverse
split. The board granted authority to effect a 1 for 10 reverse
stock split, which was effective February&nbsp;4, 2005. The
split is reflected in all share and per share information found
throughout the consolidated financial statements. In addition,
on February&nbsp;4, 2005, the number of authorized shares of
common stock was decreased to 50,000,000&nbsp;shares upon
completion of the reverse stock split.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Stock offering</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
On January&nbsp;30, 2004, the Company secured an additional
$7.5&nbsp;million, net of expenses, in funding to finance its
operations and the development of its business. The additional
funding was raised through a private placement of equity
securities consisting of 1,230,769&nbsp;shares of common stock
and five-year warrants to purchase&nbsp;615,384&nbsp;shares of
common stock at $7.20&nbsp;per share.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;

were converted into common stock as part of the terms of the
equity funding.

</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Effective April 2000, the Company&#146;s Board of Directors
approved the ServiceWare Technologies, Inc. 2000 Stock Incentive
Plan (the &#147;Plan&#148;) which amended and restated the 1996
ServiceWare, Inc. Amended and Restated Stock Option Plan (the
&#147;1996 Plan&#148;). The Plan is administered by the Board of
Directors and provides for awards of stock options to employees,
officers, directors, consultants and advisors. In May 2005, the
Company&#146;s stockholders approved amendments to the 2000
Stock Incentive Plan to: (i)&nbsp;increase the maximum aggregate
number of shares of common stock available for issuance
thereunder from 1,240,817 to 2,453,387&nbsp;shares and to
reserve an additional 1,212,570&nbsp;shares of common stock for
issuance in connection with awards granted or to be granted
under the Plan; (ii)&nbsp;approve an increase in the grant limit
under the Plan from 100,000 to 500,000&nbsp;shares per
participant per fiscal year; and (iii)&nbsp;change the name of
the Plan to &#147;Knova Software, Inc. 2000 Stock Incentive
Plan.&#148; The maximum aggregate number of shares of the
Company&#146;s Common Stock that may be subject to grants made
under the Plan is increased by any shares of Common Stock
covered by any unexercised portion of stock options granted
under the 1996 Plan that terminate after April&nbsp;11, 2005,
and also by an automatic annual increase on the first day of
each fiscal year of the Company beginning on or after
January&nbsp;1, 2006 and ending on or before December&nbsp;31,
2010 equal to the lesser of (i)&nbsp;100,000&nbsp;shares,
(ii)&nbsp;6.25% of the shares outstanding on the last day of the
immediately preceding fiscal year, or (iii)&nbsp;such lesser
number of shares as is determined by the Company&#146;s Board of
Directors.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Management and the Board of Directors determined the exercise
price of incentive stock options for the period from April 2000
through the Company&#146;s initial public offering in August
2000 (&#147;IPO&#148;). After the IPO, the exercise price of
incentive stock options is the closing market price of the
Company&#146;s Common Stock on the date of the grant. The Board
of Directors also determines the exercise price of nonqualified
options. Options generally vest over a four-year period, or over
such other period as the Board of Directors determines, and may
be accelerated in the event of certain transactions such as
merger or sale of the Company. These options expire within ten
years after the date of grant. During 2005 and 2004, the Company
recorded $503,941 and $136,220 in stock based compensation
expenses related to restricted stock grants to executives and
granting options at less than market price.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Employee Stock Purchase Plan</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In May 2000, the Company adopted the Employee Stock Purchase
Plan (ESPP). Under the terms of the ESPP, the Company is
authorized to issue up to 50,000&nbsp;shares of common stock,
plus annual increases, as defined by the plan document. The ESPP
enables employees to purchase shares of the Company&#146;s
common stock at a discounted price through after-tax payroll
deductions. Shares are offered to employees in six month
offering periods. Eligible employees elect to have deducted from
1% to 15% of their base compensation. The amounts deducted can
be used to purchase the Company&#146;s common stock at the
lesser of 85% of the fair value on the first day of the offering
period or 85% of the fair value on last day of the offering
period (purchase date). As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, the
Company has discontinued the plan.
</DIV>

were excluded from historical basic and diluted loss per share
because of their antidilutive effect. The number of outstanding
options to purchase common stock for which the option exercise
price exceeded the average market price of the Company&#146;s
common stock aggregated 1,398,126, 912,105, and 94,824 for the
years ended December&nbsp;31, 2005, 2004, and 2003 respectively.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company has a 401(k) profit sharing plan (the
&#147;Plan&#148;) covering all of its employees subject to
certain age and service requirements. Under provisions of the
Plan, participants may contribute up to 15% of their eligible
compensation to the Plan. The Company contributed $42,465,
$78,995 and $26,952 in matching contributions to the Plan in
2005, 2004 and 2003, respectively. The Company no longer matches
contributions as of June&nbsp;1, 2005.

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company&#146;s investments are held in an account with an
investment firm that is related to a director of the Company and
his affiliated entities, who collectively own more than 25% of
the Company&#146;s stock. Also, as investments are purchased and
sold, this investment firm may hold a cash balance. At
December&nbsp;31, 2005 and 2004, the cash balance with this
investment firm was $89,418 and $64,926, respectively. The
maximum balance in the cash and investment accounts during 2005
and 2004 was $9,152,392 and $9,331,387, respectively.

<DIV align="center" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">

entities, who collectively owned approximately 20% of the
Company&#146;s stock prior to the acquisition of convertible

</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
An investment firm that is related to a director of the Company
served as one of the placement agents in connection with the
private placement, which occurred in January 2004, of an
aggregate of 1,230,769&nbsp;shares of the Company&#146;s common
stock and warrants to purchase up to an aggregate of
615,385&nbsp;shares of common stock. In connection with the
private placement, the investment firm received a placement fee
of $240,000. Affiliates of the investment firm purchased
$455,000 of units in the private placement. In addition,
entities with respect to which the director has or shares voting
power purchased $162,500 of units in the private placement.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; color: #000000; background: #ffffff;"></DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B>Note&nbsp;17.</B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B>Acquisition</B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
On December&nbsp;22, 2004, the Company entered into a business
combination agreement with Kanisa Inc. under which Kanisa became
a wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company through a merger with a
newly created wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company. The merger
was completed on February&nbsp;8, 2005 pursuant to an Amended
Merger Agreement. Bruce Armstrong, CEO of Kanisa, became CEO of
the Company. The corporate headquarters of the Company following
consummation of the merger is Cupertino, California.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
In pursuing this merger, the Company determined that combining
the technology, research and development resources, customer
relationship and sales and marketing capabilities of the two
companies would create a stronger and more competitive company,
with the breadth and scale that the Company&#146;s market
demands.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Pursuant to the Merger, the Kanisa stockholders received a total
of 3,501,400&nbsp;shares of $.01&nbsp;par value common stock of
the Company, which represented 40% of post-merger common stock
outstanding. Warrants to purchase an additional
423,923&nbsp;shares of common stock at an exercise price of
$7.20&nbsp;per share were issued to the Kanisa stockholders.
These warrants will expire in January 2009. The fair value of
each warrant was estimated using the Black-Scholes
option-pricing model with the following weighted average
assumptions:
</DIV>

<CENTER>
<TABLE width="100%" align="center" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="0" border="0" style="font-size: 10.0pt; margin-top: 6pt; ">

    Volatility</DIV>
    </TD>
    <TD>&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD align="left" valign="bottom">
    142.67%</TD>
</TR>

    <TD align="left" valign="top">

    Risk-free interest rate</DIV>
    </TD>

    3.4%</TD>
</TR>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The Company expensed the acquired in-process research and
development in the first quarter of 2005. The core and developed
technologies of $4.0&nbsp;million were assigned a useful life of
7&nbsp;years and the customer contracts/customer relationships
of $1.4&nbsp;million were assigned a useful life of
5&nbsp;years. These intangible assets are being amortized using
the straight-line method over their estimated useful lives.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
An investment firm, of which a Director of the Company is an
affiliate, received $350,000 in consulting fees for assistance
with the acquisition. These fees are included in the acquisition
costs.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
The merger contemplated a name change for the Company. Effective
as of May&nbsp;27, 2005, the Company&#146;s name was changed to
&#147;Knova Software, Inc.&#148;.
</DIV>

<DIV style="margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; color: #000000; background: #ffffff;"></DIV>

<TABLE width="100%" border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff;">

    <TD width="91%"></TD>

<TR valign="top">
    <TD><B><U>Item&nbsp;9A.</U></B></TD>
    <TD>
    <B><I><U>Controls and Procedures</U></I></B></TD>
</TR>

</TABLE>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
(a)&nbsp;<I><U>Evaluation of disclosure controls and
procedures</U>.</I> As of the end of the period covered by this
report, under the supervision and with the participation of our
management, including our chief executive officer
(&#147;CEO&#148;) and chief financial officer (&#147;CFO&#148;),
we evaluated the design and operation of our disclosure controls
and procedures (as defined in
Rules&nbsp;<FONT style="white-space: nowrap">13a-15(e)</FONT>
and <FONT style="white-space: nowrap">15d-15(e)</FONT> of the
Securities Exchange Act of 1934, as amended, or the
&#147;Exchange Act&#148;). Based on this evaluation, our
management, including our CEO and CFO, has concluded that our
disclosure controls and procedures are designed, and are
effective, to give reasonable assurance that the information we
are required to disclose is recorded, processed, summarized and
reported within the time periods specified in the SEC&#146;s
rules and forms. Based upon this evaluation, the CEO and CFO
concluded that our disclosure controls and procedures are
effective in providing reasonable assurance that information
required to be disclosed in our reports filed with or submitted
to the SEC under the Exchange Act is accumulated and
communicated to management, including the CEO and CFO, as
appropriate to allow timely decisions regarding required
disclosure.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
(b)&nbsp;<I><U>Changes in internal controls</U>.</I> There were
no changes in our internal control over financial reporting that
occurred during the fourth quarter of our year ended
December&nbsp;31, 2005, that have materially affected, or are
reasonably likely to materially affect, our internal control
over financial reporting.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Section&nbsp;16(a) Beneficial Ownership Reporting
Compliance</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Section&nbsp;16(a) of the Exchange Act requires our directors
and executive officers, and persons who own more than 10% of our
equity securities, to file initial reports of ownership and
reports of changes in ownership with the Securities and Exchange
Commission. Such persons are required by the Exchange Act to
furnish us with copies of all Section&nbsp;16(a) forms they file.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Based on our review of the copies of such forms received by us
with respect to transactions during 2005, or written
representations from reporting persons, we believe that all
filing requirements applicable to our directors, executive
officers and persons who own more than 10% of our equity
securities have been complied with on a timely basis except that
our new chief financial officer, Thomas Muise, filed his initial
Form&nbsp;3 one day late.
</DIV>

&#147;Code of Ethics&#148;) that applies to our principal
executive officer, principal financial officer, principal
accounting officer or controller, or persons performing similar
functions, as well as to other directors, officers and employees
of ours. The Code of Ethics is posted on our website
(www.knova.com) and is available in print free of charge to any
shareholder who

<TR valign="top">
    <TD>(1)&nbsp;</TD>
    <TD align="left">
    As Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong was first employed by us as of the merger
    with Kanisa on February&nbsp;8, 2005, the compensation shown for
    him is for the period from and after February&nbsp;8, 2005.</TD>

the lesser of $50,000 or 10% of his annual salary and bonus. The

during any of the years indicated was less than 10% of their

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Compensation Committee Interlocks and Insider
Participation</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
As of December&nbsp;31, 2005, our compensation committee
consisted of Thomas Shanahan, Thomas Unterberg and Timothy
Wallace. None of the members of our compensation committee has
ever been an officer or employee of our company. None of our
executive officers has served or serves as a member of our
compensation committee of any entity that has one or more
executive officers on our board of directors or compensation
committee. There are no, and during 2005 there were no,
compensation committee interlocks.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Employment Agreements and Change of Control Arrangements</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Bruce Armstrong</I>&nbsp;&#151; In connection with the merger
with Kanisa on February&nbsp;8, 2005, we assumed the employment
agreement between Bruce Armstrong and Kanisa under which
Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong is to serve as our president and chief
executive officer. Under the provisions of
Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong&#146;s employment agreement,
Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong is entitled to a base salary of
$250,000&nbsp;per year with a target bonus of $100,000&nbsp;per
year. Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong receives monthly draws against his
annual bonus up to $75,000 for each year and the draws are
recoverable only against bonus entitlements. Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong
is also entitled to participate in all of our standard benefit
plans. The employment agreement provides for employment at will.
Under his employment agreement, if Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong&#146;s
employment is terminated by us without cause or by
Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong for good reason (as such terms are defined
therein), Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong is entitled to a severance package
equal to one year&#146;s base salary plus 100% of his annual
bonus, all of Mr.&nbsp;Armstrong&#146;s stock options will vest
and he will have 12&nbsp;months following such termination to
exercise his stock options.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Thomas Muise</I>&nbsp;&#151; On June&nbsp;23, 2005, we
entered into an employment agreement with Thomas J. Muise under
which Mr.&nbsp;Muise is to serve as our chief financial officer.
Under the provisions of Mr.&nbsp;Muise&#146;s employment
agreement, Mr.&nbsp;Muise is entitled to a base salary of
$175,000&nbsp;per year with a target bonus of $52,500&nbsp;per
year. In addition, Mr.&nbsp;Muise has been granted options to
purchase&nbsp;88,000&nbsp;shares of stock with vesting 25% after
one year and monthly thereafter over 36&nbsp;months.
Mr.&nbsp;Muise is also entitled to participate in all of our
standard benefit plans. The employment agreement provides for
employment at will. Under his employment agreement,
Mr.&nbsp;Muise is entitled to severance pay for six months or
until he obtains other full-time employment, whichever comes
first, if his employment is terminated by us without cause or by
Mr.&nbsp;Muise for good reason.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Mark Angel</I>&nbsp;&#151; In connection with the merger with
Kanisa on February&nbsp;8, 2005, we assumed the employment
agreement between Mark Angel and Kanisa under which
Mr.&nbsp;Angel is to serve as our chief technology officer.
Under the provisions of Mr.&nbsp;Angel&#146;s employment
agreement, Mr.&nbsp;Angel is entitled to a base salary of
$175,000&nbsp;per year with a target bonus of $50,000&nbsp;per
year with bonus amounts payable quarterly. Mr.&nbsp;Angel is
also entitled to participate in all of our standard benefit
plans. The employment agreement provides for employment at will.
Under his employment agreement, if Mr.&nbsp;Angel&#146;s
employment is terminated by us without cause or by
Mr.&nbsp;Angel for good reason (as such terms are defined
therein), Mr.&nbsp;Angel is entitled to a severance package
equal to one year&#146;s base salary plus 100% of his annual
bonus, all of Mr.&nbsp;Angel&#146;s stock options will vest and
he will have 12&nbsp;months following such termination to
exercise his stock options.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
<I>Kent Heyman</I>&nbsp;&#151; In connection with the merger
with Kanisa on February&nbsp;8, 2005, we entered into an
agreement with Kent Heyman, replacing Mr.&nbsp;Heyman&#146;s
previous employment agreement with us. Under the agreement,
Mr.&nbsp;Heyman will serve as our non-executive chairman of the
board until his resignation or removal. Mr.&nbsp;Heyman will
receive his current salary of $225,000&nbsp;per year and
benefits until December&nbsp;31, 2006. Mr.&nbsp;Heyman will also
receive an additional $12,000&nbsp;per year so long as the
agreement remains in effect. In January 2006, Mr.&nbsp;Heyman
was paid $100,000, which represented his bonus commitment for
2005 and 2006. All of Mr.&nbsp;Heyman&#146;s stock options for
the purchase of our common stock vested upon the execution of
the agreement and Mr.&nbsp;Heyman will be entitled to exercise
his stock options at any time during the term of the agreement.
The consulting agreement expires on the second anniversary
following Mr.&nbsp;Heyman&#146;s resignation or removal as a
member of our board of directors.
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 12pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">
<B>Compensation of Directors</B>
</DIV>

<DIV align="left" style="font-size: 10.0pt;color: #000000; background: #ffffff; margin-top: 6pt; margin-left: 0; margin-right: 0; margin-bottom: 0; ">&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;
Our directors do not receive any cash compensation for their
services as directors, but we reimburse directors for reasonable
and necessary expenses incurred in connection with attendance at
meetings of our board of directors and other company business.
In December 2005, pursuant to our 2000 Stock Incentive Plan, we
granted to one of our outside directors options to
purchase&nbsp;10,000&nbsp;shares of our common stock. The
exercise price of these options is $3.05&nbsp;per share, and
vest over a period of 24&nbsp;months.
</DIV>
